# Korean 06 coverage matrix

## Stage outcome and completion rule

Korean 06 is the final advanced stage for contemporary standard South Korean. It bridges TOPIK 6-oriented comprehension to advanced functional production, register control, interaction, editing, mediation, and source-based communication. It makes no near-native claim.

The accepted stage contains exactly 3,200 cards in 80 cumulative generation slices of 40 cards:

- IDs: `ko-06-0001`–`ko-06-3200`.
- Immutable prerequisite source: Korean 00–05, exactly 9,780 accepted cards.
- Completed Korean 00–06 total: exactly 12,980 accepted cards.
- The 80 slices have disjoint primary burdens but form one cumulative acceptance chain.
- Exactly 3,200 cards is valid only if every listed burden is evidenced without mechanical variants, prerequisite duplication, rarity, or filler.

## Card, language, and source contract

Every card must use one original, natural complete Korean sentence or short coherent passage as its audio cue and test one focused production span. Visible context must make the answer fair. The cloze must remain within one sentence, quotation, paragraph, and speaker turn. Terminal punctuation stays outside `cloze` and `answer`; `answer` equals `cloze` exactly. Input is strict Hangul with current standard South Korean spelling and spacing. Romanization and `reading` are prohibited.

Only independently genuine contextual alternatives may appear in `accepted_answers`. English `meaning` support is concise. Optional `grammar`, `register`, and `note` fields appear only when they directly help the learner; unused fields are omitted. Internal generation, slice, review, audit, validation, index, and cloze-design language is prohibited. Every card declares `work/audio/ko-KR/06/<card-id>.mp3`.

Whole-sentence clozes are limited to compact useful fixed expressions. Long dictation, arbitrary enlarged clozes, mechanical name or number substitutions, and lightly altered Korean 00–05 material are prohibited.

## Binding per-slice position contract

Every slice binds its ID positions as follows:

- First 16 IDs: `C`, construction, ending, collocation, dense-written-form, or other linguistic-form production.
- Next 12 IDs: `F`, communicative-function or discourse-operation production.
- Next 4 IDs: `R`, relationship/register production. `R` specifically tests selection or reformulation of language for a declared audience, relationship, hierarchy, speech level, or genre; it is not a context-only label.
- Next 4 IDs: `L`, production whose fairness also depends on recovering standard connected delivery, grouping, source ownership, turn force, reduction, or sentence-final intonation.
- Last 4 IDs: `O`, production focused on standard spelling, spacing, punctuation, quotation, number notation, or phrase grouping.

Thus every row contains `C16/F12/R4/L4/O4`. All cards remain production cards and all cues receive listening review. Every slice includes spoken and written production, at least one spontaneous reformulation, and at least one relationship-sensitive choice. A card counts in exactly one primary position.

## Binding global inventory

The accepted cards must collectively distinguish advanced final stance; observation, report, inference, appearance, recollection, assumption, probability, and suspended judgment; qualification by population, time, location, condition, purpose, exception, degree, and evidential reach; concession, limitation, contrast, counterexpectation, hypothesis, counterfactuality, inevitability, constraint, threshold, cause, condition, correlation, purpose, means, consequence, comparison, substitution, temporal/aspectual control, embedded discourse, nominalization, passive and causative responsibility, cohesion, recoverable ellipsis, rhetorical organization, editing, and speech-level consistency.

Productive constructions must be contemporary and contextual. Useful forms such as `-에 불과하다`, `-에 지나지 않다`, `-기는커녕`, `-을 뿐더러`, `-는 한`, `-지 않는 한`, `-다고 치다`, `-는 셈 치다`, `-을 경우`, `-았더라면`, `-았을 텐데`, `-았어야 했다`, `-을 수밖에 없다`, `-지 않을 수 없다`, `-기 마련이다`, `-게 되어 있다`, `-을 정도로`, `-다 못해`, and `-기에 이르다` are admitted only where natural. Marginal or archaizing forms such as `-다손 치더라도` are excluded from production.

Functions include precise qualification; evidence evaluation; calibrated certainty; concession; causal and conditional reasoning; indirect disagreement, criticism, correction, requests, refusals, deferral, conditional acceptance, and negotiation; authority, delegation, escalation, accountability, and handoff; turn management, interruption, repair, backchannels, and topic shifts; emotionally appropriate speech; recoverable humor, irony, understatement, implication, and ambiguity; speeches, interviews, debates, meetings, presentations, reports, proposals, editorials, and analysis; faithful quotation, paraphrase, comparison, synthesis, mediation, editing, and spontaneous professional explanation.

Domains include workplace and organizations; education and research; public services and institutions; media and information; economy and everyday finance; technology; civic and social issues; environment, safety, and infrastructure; health and welfare communication; culture and arts; projects and negotiation; and source-based rhetorical communication. Specialist terms require general-use context. Binding Sino-Korean families include `시행/실시/수행/실행`, `검토/검증/확인/점검`, `근거/자료/사례/증거`, `결과/성과/효과/영향`, `비용/경비/지출/부담`, `감소/절감/축소/완화`, `보도/발표/공개/전달`, `협의/논의/조정/합의`, `책임/권한/소관/재량`, `추정/추측/전망/예측`, and `수정/정정/개정/보완` in natural collocations. Isolated Hanja is excluded.

Productive registers are formal spoken `합니다체`, ordinary polite `해요체`, intimate `해체` with an explicit stable relationship, neutral written `한다체`, formal institutional writing, and accessible contemporary literary or rhetorical prose. Honorification, titles, `께/께서`, `드리다`, `모시다`, `여쭙다`, `뵙다`, and lexical honorifics must match the represented relationship. Power, age, expertise, institutional role, familiarity, and setting remain distinct. Register switching requires a changed audience, quoted voice, deliberate distance, intimacy shift, or public/private boundary.

Generational, occupational, institutional, regional, and social variation is recognition-only. The learner must produce a contemporary standard South Korean paraphrase or response. A nonstandard form cannot be a primary target or accepted answer.

Pragmatic force must be recoverable from role, prior action, timing, consequence, wording, or response. Intonation may support but cannot supply the only evidence. The source distinguishes questions from criticism, suggestions from orders, refusal from deferral, conditional acceptance from reluctant agreement, concern from warning, understatement from literal minimization, irony from misinformation, humor from ridicule, and controlled ambiguity from missing context.

Orthography covers bound nouns, auxiliaries, counters, dependent constructions, names and titles, institutional compounds, standard contractions, `되/돼`, `안/않`, `데/대`, `든/던`, `로서/로써`, `맞히다/맞추다`, quotation, nested quotation, natural Korean commas and colons, parentheses, ellipses and dashes, Korean and Arabic numbers, dates, times, percentages, units, ranges, approximations, statistics, lists, and paragraph punctuation. Semicolons are used only where natural Korean prose warrants them.

Listening covers standard pronunciation cues, phrase rhythm, lawful connected delivery and reductions, final intonation, source ownership, quotation boundaries, qualifications, enumerations, numbers, contrast, rapid group interaction, separate-turn interruption and repair, controlled emotion, presentation cadence, interview follow-up, debate challenge, and spontaneous explanation. A cloze never crosses speakers, and simulated overlap cannot obscure the target.

## Accepted-primary-target coverage index

This repository coverage table is synchronized after each accepted Korean 06 card. It is designed for exactly one row per accepted Korean 06 card; Korean 00–05 remain immutable prerequisite indexes rather than duplicated rows in this file. Rows may and must be appended as slices are accepted; corrections update the existing card row rather than creating a second primary row.

The executor populates and synchronizes this table with the card source. Acceptance evidence records whether every optional accepted answer was validated for the same visible context, meaning, spelling, and register; accepted answers are not additional primary-target rows.

The compact child-prompt index is a separate UTF-8 TSV artifact supplied in author and reviewer prompts. It contains only one line per accepted card:

`<card-id><TAB><normalized-primary-target>`

It contains Korean 00–05 and accepted earlier Korean 06 cards, sorted lexically by card ID. It does not contain kind codes, accepted-answer rows, meanings, sentences, slice labels, notes, prompts, or review material. Genuine accepted answers are validated against uniqueness and fairness but never become primary rows in either index.

## Normalization, uniqueness, and originality

Primary normalization is deterministic: Unicode NFC, compatibility-width normalization, leading/trailing whitespace removal, and collapse of internal whitespace runs to one space. Meaningful internal Korean punctuation is retained. Terminal punctuation is absent because it remains outside the target.

Every normalized primary target must be unique across Korean 00–06. Punctuation-light, spacing-light, morphology-aware, sentence-frame, and semantic diagnostics identify cosmetic or mechanical near-duplicates for editorial review; diagnostic similarity does not itself prohibit a genuinely distinct advanced burden or authorize nonstandard spelling.

An accepted answer must be independently natural in the same visible context and cannot hide a duplicate, relax standard spelling, change relationship or register, or enlarge the cloze arbitrarily. A duplicate is repaired by selecting a genuinely different focused burden and rewriting the sentence naturally. Originality checks compare Korean 00–05, accepted Korean 06, and the current slice. Copied sentences, distinctive passages, living-author imitation, mechanical lexical series, repeated sentence frames, and context-noun-only variants are rejected.

## Global exclusions

Exclude rare or archaic grammar, isolated Hanja, unexplained classical quotations, obscure proverbs, specialist jargon without general-use context, dialect imitation as production, stigmatizing sociolect caricature, mechanical ending conversion, name/value substitution, synonym lists without collocations, trivia, long dictation, near-native claims, and private-culture trick answers. Also exclude financial, legal, or medical directives; partisan persuasion; gratuitous violence; harassment; humiliation-based humor; prescriptive claims that one style is universally neutral; and any nonstandard recognition form as a primary or accepted answer.

## Per-slice generation and acceptance gates

Each slice uses two separate sequential fresh one-shot processes. The author runs first and the Korean editor runs second. Both use `codex exec --ephemeral`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, high reasoning effort, read-only sandbox, JSON-only output, no session reuse, no repository edits, and no nested in-process agents. Exactly 80 author and 80 reviewer processes are required.

Every author prompt contains the complete authoritative issue, exact row, exact IDs and positional classes, required JSON shape, cumulative accepted-card prerequisite range and count, compact primary-target TSV, locale and South Korean variety, registers and relationships, stage boundary, card contract, and exclusions. The reviewer receives the same authority and identical prerequisite TSV plus the proposed 40-card batch and returns fully corrected JSON only.

A slice is accepted only after:

1. Exactly 40 sequential IDs, fixed `C16/F12/R4/L4/O4` positions, and exact audio paths are present.
2. Schema, Hangul-only input, cloze occurrence, answer equality, terminal-punctuation boundary, optional metadata, and speaker-turn boundaries pass.
3. Naturalness, grammar, particles, inflection, viewpoint, cohesion, register, hierarchy, honorification, pragmatic force, source ownership, spelling, spacing, and answer fairness pass independent Korean editing.
4. Every declared burden is evidenced by its assigned card rather than inferred from metadata.
5. Source validation, normalized-primary-target uniqueness, accepted-answer review, originality diagnostics, cloze-boundary checks, metadata checks, and Korean 06 stage-boundary checks pass.
6. Cards, the repository coverage index, and the cumulative compact prompt index are synchronized after acceptance.
7. Several representative complete-sentence MP3s, including an `L` card, an `O` or numeric card, and a longer connected cue, are generated with the current fixed pipeline.
8. Every representative file exists, is nonempty, completely decodes, has positive duration, and reports 48 kHz mono output. Defects are corrected and affected samples regenerated before proceeding.
9. No per-slice human listening occurs. Objective representative-audio validation is the slice gate; human listening occurs once at the final blocking stage.
10. No prompt store, transcript archive, review framework, workflow engine, or generation artifact enters tracked source.

The acceptance chain is `K00–05 → S01 → S02 → … → S80`. If the executor reaches a process or thread limit, it may create a local checkpoint commit and stop without discarding accepted work. Nothing is pushed until one coherent work round is ready.

## Ten final focused reviews

After all 3,200 cards are slice-accepted, run ten additional fresh one-shot Korean-editor processes with the same fixed model, effort, sandbox, JSON-only, no-reuse, and no-repository-edit rules:

1. Advanced grammar, endings, qualification, certainty, evidence, and stance.
2. Honorification, hierarchy, speech level, relationship context, and register switching.
3. Indirect meaning, disagreement, criticism, requests, refusals, correction, negotiation, irony, and understatement.
4. Rapid interaction, interruption, turn management, repair, backchannels, topic shifts, emotion, and conversational naturalness.
5. Formal speech, interviews, debates, meetings, presentations, professional explanation, and Q&A.
6. Reports, proposals, academic, institutional, media, editorial, source fidelity, paraphrase, comparison, and synthesis.
7. Accessible literary and rhetorical language, idioms, cultural references, humor, originality, and absence of archaism.
8. Spelling, spacing, punctuation, numbers, Sino-Korean collocations, word families, terminology, and word choice.
9. Cloze scope, answer fairness, optional metadata, accepted answers, normalized-target uniqueness, and originality.
10. Complete Korean 00–06 boundary, prerequisite preservation, cumulative regression, exclusions, inventory accounting, and absence of padding.

Each process receives the complete authority and relevant source and returns fully corrected source JSON, not commentary. Every verified correction is applied before final audio generation. All ten areas must finish with zero unresolved blocking findings.

## Final source, audio, archive, and listening gates

After focused corrections:

- Rerun source validation for Korean 00–06 and normalized-primary-target uniqueness across exactly 12,980 cards.
- Generate all 3,200 Korean 06 MP3s on the designated server using the unchanged fixed Korean reference pair and active pipeline: VoxCPM2 Ultimate untreated waveform → `MossFormer2_SE_48K` → validated 48 kHz mono waveform → MP3.
- Do not change the model, denoiser, reference pair, CLI, encoding, audio architecture, or retained environments.
- Run `python meiki_decks.py check --language ko-KR --stage 06` and `python meiki_decks.py check --language ko-KR --stage 06 --require-audio`.
- Verify exactly 3,200 audio files with correct paths, nonzero bytes, complete decode, positive duration, 48 kHz, mono, and valid hashes, with zero failures.
- Run source checks for every Korean stage 00–06, `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -v`, `python3 -m compileall -q meiki_decks.py mossformer2_batch.py tests`, the repository formatting check, and `git diff --check`. CI must not load speech models or require generated audio.
- Delete only the existing `dist/meiki-ko-kr-complete-v0.1.0.meiki`, rebuild it, reopen it, and verify lexical stage order 00–06, exactly 12,980 cards, deck/note/card/media/relationship/schedule integrity, paths, byte sizes, media hashes, ZIP entry count, collection checksum, and archive SHA-256.
- Confirm audio, WAVs, archive, models, references, caches, environments, logs, prompts, and temporary outputs remain untracked.
- Copy five objectively validated Korean 06 samples and a plain manifest to `~/Downloads/meiki-ko-KR-06-samples/`: one indirect question/request/disagreement; one number/date/statistic/formal-data cue; one ordinary polite or intimate interaction; one formal speech/meeting/presentation; and one long connected analytical or source-based cue.
- Human listening is a single final blocking review after objective validation, CI, and orchestrator review pass.

## Completion accounting and 3,200-card justification

| Block | Slices | Cards | Binding burden |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| Stance, qualification, evidence | S01–S10 | 400 | scope, certainty, evidentiality, evidence quality, evaluation, limitation, correction, integrated stance |
| Concession and reasoning | S11–S20 | 400 | concession, contrast, conditions, assumptions, counterfactuals, inevitability, thresholds, cause, purpose, consequence |
| Indirect professional meaning | S21–S30 | 400 | questions, disagreement, criticism, correction, requests, refusal, deferral, conditional acceptance, negotiation |
| Interaction and pragmatics | S31–S40 | 400 | turns, interruption, repair, backchannels, shifts, emotion, apology, humor, irony, implication, recognition-only variation |
| Hierarchy and spoken genres | S41–S50 | 400 | honorification, delegation, approval, escalation, handoff, speeches, interviews, debates, meetings, presentations |
| Written genres and sources | S51–S60 | 400 | reports, proposals, academic, institutional, media, editorial, paraphrase, comparison, synthesis, mediation |
| Domains and rhetoric | S61–S70 | 400 | required domains, Sino-Korean families, technical mediation, culture, accessible literary/rhetorical prose |
| Editing and capstones | S71–S80 | 400 | grammar, concision, register, orthography, punctuation, mediation, interaction, genres, synthesis, stage-boundary integration |
| **Total** | **S01–S80** | **3,200** | **Every card has a named disjoint primary burden** |

The positional totals are `C 1,280`, `F 960`, `R 320`, `L 320`, and `O 320`, totaling 3,200. The count is the lower planning bound because the rows allocate substantive production separately: 800 cards distinguish stance and reasoning; 800 bind indirect communication and interaction; 800 bind hierarchy, spoken/written genres, and source operations; 400 bind advanced domains and lexical families; and 400 bind editing, orthography, audience mediation, and explicitly defined capstones.

S80 is not a residual reserve. Its 40 positions have named boundary and integration burdens and cannot absorb an unspecified gap. If any global requirement lacks accepted evidence, the proposed source is incomplete and must be corrected within the relevant assigned row or, if that is genuinely impossible without displacing another burden, documented as requiring an additional named slice rather than padded into these IDs. Conversely, no card may be added merely to reach or exceed a count.

Completion requires all 80 author/editor pairs, ten focused reviews, source and uniqueness checks, 3,200 objectively valid MP3s, verified Korean 00–06 archive, green CI, final sample set, blocking human listening pass, exactly one `Fixes #96` in the pull-request description, and no uncovered or unresolved requirement.
